Projection code:
def project_box_mean(v, low, high, target):
    v = np.asarray(v)
    # If sum already within and bounds satisfied after clip, adjust single? First clip.
    # Determine λ range: f(λ)=sum(clip(v - λ,low,high)). f decreasing in λ. Need f=target.
    lam_lo = (v.min() - high) - 1? If λ very low, clip high. Choose lo = min(v)-high - 1? high-low length, f decreasing. We can choose lo = v.min() - high - 1 => v-lam >=1+high? Actually if λ = min(v)-high -1, v_i - λ >=1+high - v_i? For i min, = high+1 => clip high. So f= n*high. hi = max(v)-low +1 => v_i - λ <= low-1, f=n*low.
    if target > f(lo) or <f(hi): target unreachable? just clip.
    bisection 100 iters.
    q=clip(v-lambda, low, high)
    residual=target-q.sum()
    if abs(residual)>1e-15:
       # find indices with slack: if residual>0, q_i<high; else q_i>low
       candidates = np.where((q < high-1e-12) if residual>0 else (q>low+1e-12))[0]
       if len>0:
          idx=candidates[0]
          # ensure can absorb residual within bounds
          room = high-q[idx] if residual>0 else q[idx]-low
          add= residual if abs(residual)<=room else (room if residual>0 else -room)
          q[idx]+=add
    return clip(q,low,high)

### q objective and gradient functions
Top-level functions:
```
def project_box_mean(v, low, high, target_sum): ...
def project_q(q, n): return project_box_mean(q,-1,1,0)
def h_from_q(q): return 0.5*(q+1)
def compute_cmax_q(q,n):
   q=project_q(q,n)
   S=np.correlate(q,q,'full')
   idx=np.arange(2*n-1) - (n-1)
   d=np.abs(idx)
   N=n-d
   prefix=np.empty(n+1); prefix[0]=0; prefix[1:]=np.cumsum(q)
   T=np.empty(n)
   # T[0]=0; for d>0: prefix[n-d]+prefix[d]
   T = prefix[n - np.arange(n)] + prefix[np.arange(n)]  # check array indices: arange n -> 0..n-1; n-arange -> n..1, valid prefix len n+1.
   c=(N - S + np.sign(idx)*T[np.minimum(d,n-1)?])/ (2*n) # d from 0 to n-1; T size n; okay.
   c[n-1]=(n - S[n-1])/(2*n) # T[0]=0, sign0=0; formula works too.
   maxc=c.max()
   return float(maxc), c
```

```
def grad_lag_h(h,n,k,dx):
  grad=np.zeros(n)
  if k==0: grad += (1-2*h)*dx  # derivative h-h² =1-2h
  elif k>0:
    nv=n-k
    grad[:nv] += (1 - h[k:]) * dx
    grad[k:] += (-h[:nv]) * dx
  else:
    d=-k; nv=n-d
    grad[:nv] += (-h[d:]) * dx
    grad[d:] += (1 - h[:nv]) * dx
  return grad
```

```
else: sum(h*(1-h))*dx
```

Gradient for active lags in h remains as earlier for f orientation. Let's define:
```
def lag_grad_h(h,n,k, dx):
  g=np.zeros(n)
  if k==0:
    g = (1-2*h)*dx
  elif k>0:
    nv=n-k
    g[:nv] += (1-h[k:]) * dx
    g[k:] += -h[:nv] * dx
  else:
    d=-k; nv=n-d
    g[:nv] += -h[d:] * dx
    g[d:] += (1-h[:nv]) * dx
  return g
```
This is exact derivative of f_k. For smooth max weights, grad =Σ w*lag_grad + penalty.

```
def compute_cvec_h(h,n):
  # assumes h length n, maybe not exact mean; does not clip? Use given.
  q=2*h-1.0
  Q=float(q.sum())
  # use np.dot for q dot? correlate
  S = np.correlate(q, q, mode='full')
  idx = np.arange(2*n-1) - (n-1)
  d = np.abs(idx)
  N = n - d
  c = np.empty_like(S, dtype=float)
  center=n-1
  c[center] = (n - S[center])/(2.0*n)
  mask = np.arange(2*n-1) != center
  if n > 1:
    dm = d[mask]
    Km = idx[mask]
    # P
    P = np.empty(n+1); P[0]=0; P[1:]=np.cumsum(q)
    Pnd = P[n - dm]
    Pd = P[dm]
    val = N[mask] - S[mask]
    pos = Km > 0
    neg = Km < 0
    c[mask & pos] = (val[pos_indices?] + Pnd[pos] + Pd[pos] - Q)/(2n)
```

```
Compute active weighted gradient:
```
def active_h_grad(h,n,active,alpha,mu):
  c,cmax? Need weights. We can compute cvec once. vals=c[active]; stable weights.
  g=np.zeros(n)
  dx=2/n
  for idx,wi in zip(active, weights):
    if wi < 1e-14: continue
    k=idx-(n-1)
    if k==0: lag=(1-2h)*dx
    elif k>0: lag=zeros; lag[:n-k]+=(1-h[k:])*dx; lag[k:]+= -h[:n-k]*dx
    else: d=-k; lag[:n-d]+= -h[d:]*dx; lag[d:]+= (1-h[:n-d])*dx
    g += wi*lag
  g += 2*mu*(h.sum()-n/2)
  return g
```
But note `wi` weights sum 1? In smooth max gradient of val=mx+logsum/alpha: weights = exp(alpha(c-mx))/sum. They sum 1. Good. The gradient of the smooth max with respect to h is Σ weights ∇c_idx. No extra alpha. Good.
```

def smooth_max(cvals, alpha):
  mx=cvals.max(); sh=alpha*(cvals-mx); if sh.max()>400: val=mx+(smx+logsumexp)/alpha else ...
```

We can implement `select_active(c, top_r, tol_frac)`:
```
cmax=c.max()
threshold=cmax - max(1e-9, tol_frac*cmax)
active=np.where(c >= threshold)[0]
if len(active)>top_r:
  active=active[np.argsort(-c[active])[:top_r]]
if len(active)==0: active=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
return active
```

Golden search:
```
def golden_minimize_1d(func, a, b, tol=1e-7, max_iter=60):
    gr=(sqrt(5)-1)/2
    p=a+ (1-gr)*(b-a); q=a+gr*(b-a)
    fp=func(p); fq=func(q)
    for ...:
      if fp < fq: b=q; q=p; fq=fp; p=...; fp=func(p)
      else: a=p; p=q; fp=fq; q=...; fq=func(q)
    return (a+b)/2
```
Function not convex? Golden still converges to local if unimodal. We can also sample endpoints and old, choose best before. Since c0 concave may create local; we can try ternary from 0 to1 maybe. Compute slope vector top-level:
```
def lag_slope_vec(i,h,n):
  L=2*n-1; s=np.zeros(L); dx=2/n; c=n-1
  if i+1 < n:
     k=np.arange(1,n-i)
     s[c+k] = (1.0 - h[i+1:n])*dx
  if i>0:
     k=np.arange(1,i+1)
     s[c-k] = (-h[i-1::-1])*dx
  return s
```
Check lengths: for i=n-1, n-i=1, arange(1,1) empty; fine.
For h_i current old, slope coefficients use neighbors current h. If z changes, coefficients independent of z for k≠0 (since products h_i*h_j with h_j fixed). Good.
```

def project_box_mean(v, low, high, target):
    v=np.asarray(v,dtype=float).copy()
    if not np.isfinite(v).all(): ...
    # quick if target within bounds and sum nearly?
    lo = float(v.min()) - high - 1.0
    hi = float(v.max()) - low + 1.0
    # f(lam)=sum(clip(v-lam))
    # Need decreasing. lo gives high, hi gives low.
    if target >= n*high: return np.full(n, high)
    if target <= n*low: return np.full(n, low)
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       s=np.clip(v-mid, low, high).sum()
       if s > target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    lam=(lo+hi)/2
    q=np.clip(v-lam, low, high)
    res=target-q.sum()
    # adjust
    if abs(res)>1e-14:
       if res>0:
          idx=np.flatnonzero(q < high - 1e-12)
          if len: 
             room=high-q[idx[0]]; add=min(res,room); q[idx[0]]+=add
       else: ...
    return q
```
For nearly all at bounds, target might require fractional at one; bisection should leave some interior unless target at extreme. If bisection λ makes all at bounds due to flat? Actually v has continuous values; sum piecewise linear with flat only when all clipped. For target not extreme, bisection to 1e-16 leaves interior. Adjustment fine.
Could avoid 80 iterations; 60 enough. Use vector sum each iter. For n=4096*60=245k ops, okay. If called 1000 times 245M, maybe. We can use sorting to exact O(n log n) but bisection okay. Maybe use 50 iters.

Alternatively, use iterative waterfill only one? Bisection robust.
```

def coordinate_descent_h(h,n,deadline, mu=1e-3, max_passes=..., grid=13, gold=25):
  h=project_h(h,n); c,cmax=compute_cvec_h(h,n); sumh=h.sum()
  best_c_exact=eval(c)
  cmax_internal = max(c)
  max_iters = max(5, int((deadline-time.time())* (1000/n?))) maybe.
  passes = 0
  improved=0
  while time < deadline-5 and passes<max_passes:
    order = rng? Need rng passed or fixed? Top-level no closures, can use global rng? Better pass `seed` or use deterministic order. Use `np.random.default_rng`? We can create rng in run and pass to coordinate; top-level functions can take random state.
    for i in order:
       if time > deadline: break
        old=h[i]
        s=lag_slope_vec(i,h,n)
        best_z = coordinate_search(...)
        if abs(best_z-old)>1e-12:
           # update c and h
           diff=best_z-old
           c += diff*s
           c[center] += dx*(best_z*(1-best_z)-old*(1-old))
           h[i]=best_z; sumh += diff
           # optional if c.max() > previous + 1e-8? continue anyway
    # end pass
    h=project_h(h,n)
    c,cmax=compute_cvec_h(h,n)
    exact=eval_cmax_h(h,n)
    if exact < best_c_exact: update
    passes+=1
  return best_h,best_c
```
Need coordinate_search not use random; maybe deterministic grid. Use old and extremes.
```

```
### project_box_mean robust:
```
def project_box_mean(v, low, high, target):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0: return v.copy()
    low=float(low); high=float(high); target=float(target)
    # nan safe
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.5*(low+high), posinf=high, neginf=low)
    # if already feasible
    if np.all(v >= low-1e-15) and np.all(v <= high+1e-15):
        s = v.sum()
        if abs(s-target) <= 1e-13:
            return v
        # adjust anyway
    # If target unreachable due to floating? clamp
    minsum = n*low; maxsum = n*high
    if target <= minsum: return np.full(n, low)
    if target >= maxsum: return np.full(n, high)
    v_clip? We can use v for f.
    lo = float(v.min()) - high - 1.0
    hi = float(v.max()) - low + 1.0
    # If f(lo) < target? adjust lo lower; f(hi) > target? adjust hi higher.
    for _ in range(5):
       sl = np.clip(v - lo, low, high).sum()
       if sl >= target: break
       lo -= (high-low+1)
    for _ in range(5):
       sh = np.clip(v - hi, low, high).sum()
       if sh <= target: break
       hi += (high-low+1)
    for _ in range(70):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       s=np.clip(v-mid, low, high).sum()
       if s > target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    q=np.clip(v-0.5*(lo+hi), low, high)
    res=target-q.sum()
    if abs(res)>1e-14:
       if res > 0:
          cand=np.where(q < high - 1e-13)[0]
          if cand.size:
             i=int(cand[0]); room=high-q[i]; add=res if res<room else room; q[i]+=add
       else:
          cand=np.where(q > low + 1e-13)[0]
          if cand.size:
             i=int(cand[0]); room=q[i]-low; add = -res if res > -room else -room; q[i]+=add
    return q
```
But if after adjustment residual remains due to no slack, target may be at bound; clip final.
Return `np.clip(q,low,high)`.

`project_h(h,n)`: `return project_box_mean(h,0.0,1.0,n*0.5)`.

### eval_cmax_h
```
def eval_cmax_h(h,n):
    if n != len(h): n=len(h)
    dx=2.0/n
    if n<=1: return 0.0
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full') * dx
    return float(np.max(c))
```
If h values slightly outside? project before.

### compute_cvec_h
Need efficient. Maybe use `np.correlate(q,q,mode='full')`. For n=1 trivial.
```
def compute_cvec_h(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    n=len(h)
    dx=2.0/n
    q=2.0*h-1.0
    S=np.correlate(q,q,mode='full')
    L=2*n-1
    c=np.empty(L,float)
    center=n-1
    c[center]=(n - S[center])/(2.0*n)
    if n==1: return c,c[0]
    idx=np.arange(L, dtype=int) - center
    d=np.abs(idx)
    N=n - d
    P=np.empty(n+1,float); P[0]=0.0; P[1:]=np.cumsum(q)
    Q=P[n]
    pos=idx > 0
    neg=idx < 0
    if pos.any():
       dp=d[pos]; sp=S[pos]
       c[pos]=(N[pos] + P[n-dp] + P[dp] - Q - sp)/(2.0*n)
    if neg.any():
       dn=d[neg]; sn=S[neg]
       c[neg]=(N[neg] - P[n-dn] - P[dn] + Q - sn)/(2.0*n)
    # max should be >= - small
    cmax=float(c.max())
    return c,cmax
```
For d=0, pos/neg false. For d values up to n-1; P index n-d for d=n-1 -> P[1], P[d]=P[n-1], okay. N>=1 for d≤n-1.
This vectorization allocates idx,d,N arrays length L each call. n=500 1000, fine; n=4096 8191, okay. Could cache per n to save? Top-level global dict? Not necessary.
But compute_cvec_h called many times. We can cache `idx,d,N` for n? Use global dictionary keyed n. In code:
```
_CVEC_CACHE = {}
def cvec_arrays(n):
   if n not in _CVEC_CACHE: ...
   return
```
No closures. This improves. Do it.
```
def get_lag_arrays(n):
    key=n
    arr=_CACHE.get(key)
    if arr is None:
       idx=np.arange(2*n-1, dtype=int) - (n-1)
       d=np.abs(idx)
       N=n - d.astype(float)
       pos=idx>0; neg=idx<0; center=n-1
       arr=(idx,d,N,pos,neg,center)
       _CACHE[key]=arr
    return arr
```
Use in compute and maybe grad. Good.

Potential memory for n=4096 arrays several *8191 ~ small.

### active objective/grad
`get_active_info(h,n,active,alpha)` could return c, weights. But objective and grad separate. We'll duplicate weight computation.
```
def smooth_weights_and_val(cvals, alpha):
   vals=np.asarray(cvals,float)
   mx=float(vals.max())
   if vals.size==0: return 0, None
   sh=alpha*(vals-mx)
   smx=sh.max()
   if smx > 400:
      e=np.exp(sh-smx); val=mx+(smx+np.log(e.sum()))/alpha
   else: e=np.exp(sh); val=mx+np.log(e.sum())/alpha
   w=e/e.sum(); return float(val), w
```
In objective:
```
def active_h_obj(x,n,active,alpha,mu):
   c,cmax=compute_cvec_h(x,n)
   val,w=smooth_weights_and_val(c[active],alpha)
   if mu:
      s=x.sum()-n/2
      val += 0.5*mu*s*s
   return val
```
In grad:
```
def active_h_grad(x,n,active,alpha,mu):
   c,cmax=compute_cvec_h(x,n)
   val,w=smooth_weights_and_val(c[active],alpha)
   g=np.zeros(n)
   dx=2.0/n
   center=n-1
   idx_arr,d_arr,N_arr,pos_arr,neg_arr,cen = get_lag_arrays(n)? Actually active indices map to k = idx - center.
   for t in range(active.size):
      if w[t] < 1e-15: continue
      li=int(active[t]); k=li-center; wt=w[t]
      if k==0:
         g += wt * (1.0 - 2.0*x) * dx
      elif k>0:
         nv=n-k
         tmp = g? To avoid allocate, do slices:
         g[:nv] += wt * (1.0 - x[k:]) * dx
         g[k:] += wt * (-x[:nv]) * dx
      else:
         d=-k; nv=n-d
         g[:nv] += wt * (-x[d:]) * dx
         g[d:] += wt * (1.0 - x[:nv]) * dx
   if mu: g += mu*(x.sum() - n/2)
   return g
```
Double-check k<0 formula with direct: z negative lag f_{-d}=Σ_{i=0}^{n-d-1} h_{i+d}(1-h_i). Derivative h_j: as first for j≥d: (1-h_{j-d}); as second for j<n-d: -h_{j+d}. We have g[:nv] += -x[d:], g[d:] += (1-x[:nv]) yes.
For k>0: f_k=Σ h_i(1-h_{i+k}); derivative first j<n-k: 1-h_j+k; second j≥k: -h_j-k. Yes.
Penalty gradient: F=0.5 μ (sum - n/2)^2 -> μ sum? If obj uses 0.5 mu*s*s, grad=mu*s. Yes.
If `mu` in obj and grad consistent. In previous I wrote 2mu if not half; use 0.5.

`select_active(c,top_r,tol_frac)`:
```
def select_active(c, top_r, tol_frac=0.002):
   cmax=float(c.max())
   threshold=cmax - max(1e-9, tol_frac*max(1.0,abs(cmax)))
   active=np.flatnonzero(c >= threshold)
   if active.size > top_r:
      # sort by c active descending; use argsort
      order=np.argsort(-c[active])
      active=active[order[:top_r]]
   if active.size == 0:
      active=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
   return active.astype(int)
```
Use `tol_frac=0.003`.

`optimize_h_active` more concrete:
```

def optimize_h_active(h,n,deadline, alphas, top_ks, mus, maxits, maxfun_multiplier=8):
    h=project_h(h,n)
    best_c=eval_cmax_h(h,n)
    current=h.copy(); current_c=best_c
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    idx=0
    for st in range(len(alphas)):
       rem=deadline-time.time()
       if rem < 15: break
       alpha=alphas[st]; top_r=top_ks[st]; mu=mus[st]; it=maxits[st]
       # maybe do 1 or 2 rounds
       for rnd in range(1): # maybe 2 for small n
          rem=deadline-time.time(); if rem<10: break
          cvec,_=compute_cvec_h(current,n)
          active=select_active(cvec, top_r, 0.003)
          if active.size==0: break
          total_rem=rem
          # estimate maxfun = it*5 if n>1000 else it*8
          try:
             res=minimize(active_h_obj, current, jac=active_h_grad,
                 args=(n,active,alpha,mu), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds,
                 options={'maxiter': it, 'ftol':1e-20, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxfun': it*10, 'maxls':20, 'maxcor':20})

                 current=cand; current_c=cc
                 if cc < best_c: best_c=cc
             else:
                 # maybe still update current if close? no
                 pass
          except Exception:
             pass
    return project_h(current,n), best_c  # But best_h maybe not current if didn't update; keep best_h variable.
```

### coordinate_search and descent
Top-level `DEFAULT_COORD_GRID`? We'll create inside `coordinate_descent_h` with `grid_base = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 13)`.
`coord_eval` requires `target`, `sum_rest`, `mu`. If `mu==0`, skip penalty.
```
def coord_eval(z, c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu):
    diff=z-old
    if diff == 0.0:
       val = float(c.max())
    else:
       arr = c + diff*s
       # update center
       arr[center] = c[center] + dx*(z*(1.0-z) - old*(1.0-old))
       val = float(arr.max())
    if mu:
       ssum=sum_rest + z - target
       val += 0.5*mu*ssum*ssum
    return val
```
If diff !=0, arr new. If diff 0, c.max each eval costly if called many; but grid includes old maybe. We can keep cmax_base in search? `coord_eval` can't. We can pass current cmax? Not necessary.
But if many evals with diff=0 (old maybe grid point), okay.

`coordinate_search` no closures:
```

def coordinate_search(c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, grid):
    # add old and near old to grid
    zs = np.unique(np.concatenate([grid, np.array([old])]))
    # maybe if old in (0,1) add old +/- 0.1
    for delta in (0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1):
       z=old+delta; if 0<z<1: append
       z=old-delta; if 0<z<1: append
    zs = ... unique in range
    vals = np.empty(len(zs))
    for i,z in enumerate(zs): vals[i]=coord_eval(z,...)
    bi=int(np.argmin(vals))
    best_z=zs[bi]; best_val=vals[bi]
    # find bracket around bi using zs? if bi interior, a=zs[bi-1], b=zs[bi+1]; else a/b from next/prev
    a,b = ...
    if b-a > 1e-8:
       # golden for up to 40
       gr=0.6180339887498949
       c1 = a + (1-gr)*(b-a); c2 = a + gr*(b-a)
       f1=coord_eval(c1,...); f2=coord_eval(c2,...)
       for _ in range(40):
          if f1 < f2:
             b=c2; c2=c1; f2=f1; c1=a+(1-gr)*(b-a); f1=eval(c1)
          else:
             a=c1; c1=c2; f1=f2; c2=a+gr*(b-a); f2=eval(c2)
       zm=0.5*(a+b); fm=eval(zm)
       if fm < best_val: best_z=zm; best_val=fm
    # final check old value? Already.
    return float(best_z)
```

`lag_slope_vec` creates zero array L each coordinate. Could optimize by using `s = np.zeros(2*n-1)` and fill. Use local variables.
```
def lag_slope_vec(i,h,n):
    L=2*n-1; s=np.zeros(L); c=n-1; dx=2.0/n
    if i+1 < n:
       s[c+1:c+n-i] = (1.0 - h[i+1:n])*dx  # length n-i-1? c+1 to c+n-i inclusive? Need slice end exclusive.
```
Let's check: For i=0,n=5, j>i: j=1..4, k=1..4, indices 5,6,7,8. `s[c+1:c+n-i]` c+1=5, c+n-i=c+5=10? exclusive 10 gives 5..9 too many (length5). We need length n-i-1 =4, indices c+1 to c+n-i-? max k=n-i-1=4, index c+4=9? Wait c=4, L=9 indices0..8. k=4 index8. `c+n-i-1?` c+4=8. End exclusive 9. So slice `c+1 : c+n-i` (4+1=5 to 4+5=9) gives indices5,6,7,8 length4. But `h[i+1:n]` length n-i-1=4. Good. For i=0,n=2: c=1, slice 2:3 length1 index2; indices 0..2, k=1 index2. End c+n-i=1+2=3 okay.
For negative: j<i: k=i-j=1..i, indices c-1 down to c-i. We can do `s[c-i:c] = (-h[i-1::-1])*dx`? Need assignment reverse. For i=4,c=4: indices 0,1,2,3 correspond k=4,3,2,1? `c-i:c` =0:4 gives [0,1,2,3]. Need values for k=1 (j=3) at index3: -h[3]; k=2 index2: -h[2]; k=3 index1:-h[1]; k=4 index0:-h[0]. So values should be `(-h[i-1::-1])` (h[3],h[2],h[1],h[0]). Assign to `s[c-i:c]` yields index0 h3? Wrong. We need reverse: value at index c-k = -h[i-k]. For slice increasing indices 0..c-1 correspond k=i..1? Let's use vector assignment with symmetric:
```
if i>0:
    s[c - np.arange(1, i+1)] = -h[i-1::-1] * dx
```
This uses advanced indexing, okay. Length i. For i=4: arange [1,2,3,4], indices [3,2,1,0], values -h[3::-1] = [-h3,-h2,-h1,-h0] -> index3 -h3, index2 -h2, correct. Good.
Positive can also use advanced indexing:
```
if i+1<n: s[c + np.arange(1,n-i)] = (1-h[i+1:n])*dx
```
Arrays small. Use advanced to avoid slice confusion.

### pair_gradient_polish
Maybe not needed with coordinate. But can add a simple local improvement after coordinate:
Use active gradient with smooth weights and pair transfers. But coordinate descent already does coordinate-wise exact. Pair moves (changing two coords) can decrease even if each coordinate move alone not. Coordinate descent may be stuck due to equality/box? It can move one coordinate while penalty handles mean; pair moves may help. We can implement a lightweight pair swap using global gradient like previous.

`pair_exchange_polish(h,n,deadline, step_sizes=[...], top_r=16, cand=12)`:
- h=project; c=eval; best.
- For step in sizes (from 1e-3 down to 1e-6):
   while time and improved:
      cvec,_=compute_cvec_h(h,n); active=select...
      g=active_h_grad(h,n,active,alpha=1000,mu=0.0)  # approximate subgradient
      # find i candidates where can increase (h<1-step), j where can decrease (h>step)
      inc = np.where(h < 1-step)[0]; dec = np.where(h > step)[0]
      if neither break
      # sort g: low g to increase? Direction d: inc i +step, dec j -step. directional derivative g_i - g_j. Want negative -> g_i < g_j.
      # choose top K smallest g from inc, top K largest g from dec.
      i_idx = inc[np.argsort(g[inc])[:K]]; j_idx = dec[np.argsort(-g[dec])[:K]]
      best_improved=False
      for i in i_idx:
         amt=min(step,1-h[i])
         for j in j_idx:
            if i==j: continue
            if h[j] >= amt:
               h[i]+=amt; h[j]-=amt
               # sum preserved
               ctry=eval_cmax_h(h,n)  # expensive original O(n²)
               if ctry < best_c - 1e-15:
                  best_c=ctry; best=True
               else: revert
      if not best: break
   # after finish, project h maybe
```
This uses eval_cmax_h O(n²) for every pair. Number pairs ~144 per eval; if 100 accepted steps, 14400 evals, too slow for n=2000. Use compute_cvec_h max for ctry (faster but same O). Maybe limit pairs to 20. Use n<=1000. This may squeeze.
Given coordinate already effective, pair polish optional for n=500 with small budget.
We can include conservative: if n<=800 and time left.

Alternative evaluate ctry using update of c vector for pair? We can maintain c vector and update for pair in O(n). But implementing pair slope with two coords possible. Let's not overcomplicate.

### parametric starts
Implement quickly:
```
def make_smooth_random_h(n,rng,m,amp=1.0):
   ctrl = amp * rng.randn(m)
   # maybe add linear trend
   x = np.linspace(0,2,m)
   xs = (np.arange(n)+0.5)*2/n
   h0 = np.interp(xs, x, ctrl)
   # normalize to [0,1]? We'll project
   return project_h(h0,n)
```
But h0 values may far outside; projection will saturate to 0/1 potentially. Better scale ctrl to [-1,1] using tanh: `ctrl = 2*np.tanh(rng.randn(m))`; `h0=0.5*(1+interp)`. Project.
Periodic:
```
def make_periodic_h(n,rng,period_choices):
  T = rng.choice(periods) # e.g. 2,3,4,6,8
  w=2*np.pi/T
  a1 = rng.uniform(0.3,1.0); p1=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
  a2 maybe...
  q = a1*np.cos(w*x+p1) + 0.5*a1*np.sin(2w*x+p2) ...
  q = q - q.mean() # for sample
  # clip to [-1,1] via q/q.max()
  qmax=max(1, np.max(np.abs(q)))
  q/=qmax
  h=0.5*(1+q)
  return project_h(h,n)
```
This produces low-frequency.

Binary starts:
```
def make_random_binary_h(n,rng):
  h=np.zeros(n); idx=rng.choice(n,n//2,replace=False); h[idx]=1; return project_h(h,n)
```
Beatty start:
```
def make_beatty_h(n,beta):
  h=np.zeros(n); pos=set(); for k in range(1, 2*n): pos.add(int(k*beta)%n); if len==n//2 break; for p in pos h[p]=1; project
```
Could be useful. Use beta=0.3819? random.

Generate starts in run:
```
def generate_starts(n, rng, parent_h=None, count=20):
    starts=[]
    if parent_h is not None:
       starts.append(upsample_h(parent_h,n))
    starts.append(make_random_binary_h(n,rng))
    starts.append(make_beatty_h(n, 0.381966))
    for m in [8,12,16,24,32,48]:
       starts.append(make_smooth_random_h(n,rng,m))
    for _ in range(4): starts.append(make_periodic_h(n,rng))
    return starts
```
Evaluate each: `c=eval_cmax_h`; keep top list. For n=2048, 12 eval*4M=48M fine.

`upsample_h`:
```
def upsample_h(h0,n):
   n0=len(h0)
   x0=(np.arange(n0)+0.5)*2.0/n0
   x1=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
   h=np.interp(x1,x0,h0, left=h0[0], right=h0[-1])
   return project_h(h,n)
```

Let's implement a very small pair polish:
```
def pair_exchange_polish(h,n,deadline, step_sizes=None, top_r=16, cand=6, max_evals=5000):
  if n>1000: return
  if step_sizes is None: step_sizes=[0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001,0.0005,0.0002,0.0001]
  h=project; best_c=eval; best_h=h
  evals=0
  for step in step_sizes:
    improved=True
    improved_count=0
    while improved and time.time()<deadline-2 and evals<max_evals:
      improved=False; improved_count=0
      cvec,_=compute_cvec_h(h,n)
      active=select_active(cvec,top_r,0.005)
      g=active_h_grad(h,n,active,alpha=5000,mu=0.0)
      inc=np.where(h < 1-step)[0]; dec=np.where(h > step)[0]
      if inc.size==0 or dec.size==0: break
      ginc=g[inc]; gdec=g[dec]
      i_list=inc[np.argsort(ginc)[:cand]]
      j_list=dec[np.argsort(-gdec)[:cand]]
      for i in i_list:
        if time.time()>deadline or evals>=max_evals: break
        amt=min(step,1-h[i])
        for j in j_list:
          if i==j: continue
          if h[j] < amt: continue
          h[i]+=amt; h[j]-=amt; evals+=1
          ctry=eval_cmax_h(h,n)
          if ctry < best_c - 1e-15:
             best_c=ctry; best_h=h.copy(); improved=True; improved_count+=1
          else:
             h[i]+= -amt; h[j]+=amt
      if improved_count==0: break
  return best_h,best_c
```
This uses original eval each pair; max_evals 5000 at n=500 maybe 5000*250k=1.25B too high? But time cap. Maybe max_evals=800. Use only if time>100. Limit.

Alternatively use `compute_cvec_h` max for ctry faster? Same correlate but maybe less 1-h? Similar. Use `eval_fast_cmax(c?)` But we need updated c. Could use c vector update for two points? Too complex. Use active_h for evaluation? no.
Maybe skip.

Now, exact final: after all, `h_final = project_h(best_h,best_n); c_final=eval_cmax_h(h_final,best_n)`. Maybe if `c_final > 0.4`? no.
Ensure `best_n` matches len. Return.

For real q, S = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(q, 2*n) * np.fft.rfft(q, 2*n), 2*n) maybe? Actually autoconv: F = rfft(q, 2*n); conv = irfft(F * F.conj(), 2*n)[:2*n-1]. For real, F*F.conj()=abs(F)^2. But convolution q*q full. `np.fft.irfft(F*F.conj(), n=2*n)`. Need small imaginary. This is O(n log n), much faster for n=4096. For n=500 direct may be faster. We can choose if n>=2048 use FFT. Need ensure S length 2n-1 and index n-1+k. Convolution index for q*q: conv[l] = Σ_{i} q[i] q[l-i]. For center l=n-1, Σ q[i] q[n-1-i] not q[i]²? Wait convolution of q with q, not correlation. Autocorrelation q with q reversed? For autocorr S_k=Σ q[i]q[i+k]. This is cross-correlation, not convolution with same. To get via FFT, convolve q and reversed q. Let `rev_q = q[::-1]`; convolve(q, rev_q). Full convolution index? For l = n-1+k, sum q[i] rev_q[l-i] = q[i] q[n-1 - (l-i)] = q[i] q[n-1-(n-1+k - i)] = q[i] q[i-k]. That gives S_{-k}. Symmetric. So use reversed.
Or use `np.correlate(q, q[::-1], 'full')`? For autocorr, np.correlate(q, q[::-1]) maybe. Simpler direct for reliability. Maybe use `np.correlate(q, q[::-1], mode='full')` and reverse? Not worth.
Numpy `correlate(q,q,'full')` is direct C optimized. We'll not FFT.

#### smooth_weights_and_val
If `vals` size 0: return 0, empty. But active nonempty.
```
def smooth_max_val_vals(vals, alpha):
    vals = np.asarray(vals, dtype=float)
    if vals.size == 0: return 0.0, None
    mx = float(vals.max())
    sh = alpha * (vals - mx)
    smx = float(sh.max())
    if smx > 400:
       e = np.exp(sh - smx)
       val = mx + (smx + float(np.log(e.sum()))) / alpha
    else:
       e = np.exp(sh)
       val = mx + float(np.log(e.sum())) / alpha
    w = e / e.sum()
    return val, w
```
Note if smx>400 shift subtract smx, logsum = smx + log(sum exp(sh-smx)). Good.

#### active_h_obj
```
def active_h_obj(x,n,active,alpha,mu):
   c,_ = compute_cvec_h(x,n)
   val, _ = smooth_max_val_vals(c[active], alpha)
   if mu:
      s = x.sum() - 0.5*n
      val += 0.5*mu*s*s
   return float(val)
```
If x outside bounds? L-BFGS may clip? We compute raw; okay.

#### active_h_grad
Need use `get_lag_arrays` maybe not; k from active index - center.
```
def active_h_grad(x,n,active,alpha,mu):
   c,_ = compute_cvec_h(x,n)
   _, w = smooth_max_val_vals(c[active], alpha)
   g = np.zeros(n,float)
   if w is None: return g
   dx=2.0/n; center=n-1
   for t in range(active.size):
      wt=float(w[t])
      if wt <= 1e-15: continue
      k = int(active[t]) - center
      if k == 0:
         g += wt * (1.0 - 2.0*x) * dx
      elif k > 0:
         nv = n - k
         g[:nv] += wt * (1.0 - x[k:]) * dx
         g[k:] += -wt * x[:nv] * dx
      else:
         d = -k; nv = n - d
         g[:nv] += -wt * x[d:] * dx
         g[d:] += wt * (1.0 - x[:nv]) * dx
   if mu:
      g += mu * (x.sum() - 0.5*n)
   return g
```
Check k>0 slice `x[k:]` length n-k = nv; `x[:nv]`. Good.
For k<0: `x[d:]` length n-d=nv, `x[:nv]`. Good.

```
def compute_cvec_h(h,n):
   h=np.asarray(h,float)
   n=len(h)
   if n==1:

      return c, float(c[0])
   idx,d,N,pos,neg,center = get_lag_arrays(n)
   q=2*h-1; S=np.correlate(q,q,mode='full')
   P=...; Q=P[n]

   if pos.any(): ...
```

#### Coordinate slope vector corrected
```
def lag_slope_vec(i, h, n):
    s = np.zeros(2*n-1, float)
    c = n-1
    dx = 2.0/n
    if i + 1 < n:
       dpos = np.arange(1, n - i, dtype=int)
       hpos = h[i+1:n]
       s[c + dpos] += (1.0 - hpos) * dx
       s[c - dpos] += -hpos * dx
    if i > 0:
       dneg = np.arange(1, i + 1, dtype=int)
       hneg = h[i-1::-1]
       s[c + dneg] += -hneg * dx
       s[c - dneg] += (1.0 - hneg) * dx
    return s
```
Check advanced indexing: `c+dpos` and `c-dpos` arrays; repeated? No unique. `+=` with advanced indexing: In numpy, `s[indices] += values` works but if indices repeated may not accumulate; here unique within each assignment, but could an index be hit by both assignments? For i=some, positive and negative assignments may overlap? For d same, c+d and c-d distinct unless d=0. Between `s[c+dpos]` and `s[c-dneg]` could overlap? They are opposite lags, might equal if c+d = c-d' => d=-d' no. Between `s[c+dpos]` and `s[c+dneg]` could overlap! positive j>i contributes to c+d; negative j<i contributes to c+d as -h. These are different d values? For a given coordinate, dpos range 1..n-1-i, dneg range1..i. They may overlap if both ranges include same d? They correspond to different neighbors at same distance left and right. Both should add to same c+d index (one from right neighbor as first? Wait if j>i with d, contribution to c+d is (1-h_right). If j<i with d, contribution to c+d is -h_left. Both correspond to different lags? f_{+d} includes sum over all i' such that i'+d valid: h_{i'}(1-h_{i'+d}). For fixed coordinate i, if there is both left and right neighbor at distance d, c+d gets contribution from coordinate i as second for left neighbor? and as first for right neighbor? Yes both. So same index gets two contributions. Since we use separate `+=` with advanced indexing, if the two `s[c + ...]` statements are separate, first assigns for right, second adds for left? In numpy, `arr[indices] += values` with unique indices within each statement works. Across statements, addition accumulates because array modified. Good. But within a statement, indices unique. So okay.
`c - dpos` and `c - dneg` similarly for opposite lag both contributions? f_{-d} includes h_{i'+d}(1-h_{i'}); coordinate i as first for left? and second for right? Both add. Separate statements accumulate. Good.

Could an index be assigned twice in the same statement due to overlap of dpos? No arange unique. Good.

But advanced `+=` has a subtlety: if indices array has duplicates, updates may not be counted multiple in one statement; no duplicates. Fine.

#### coordinate_search/grid
Need avoid `np.unique` on unsorted maybe okay.
```
def coordinate_search(c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, grid):
    zs = grid.copy()
    extras = [old]
    for dlt in (0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2):
        for zz in (old+dlt, old-dlt):
            if 0<zz<1: extras.append(zz)
    zs = np.unique(np.concatenate((zs, np.array(extras))))
    vals = np.empty(zs.size)
    for k, z in enumerate(zs): vals[k] = coord_eval(...)
    bi = int(np.argmin(vals)); best_z=float(zs[bi]); best_val=vals[bi]
    # bracket
    if bi == 0:
       a=0.0; b=float(zs[1]) if zs.size>1 else 1.0
    elif bi == zs.size-1:
       a=float(zs[-2]) if zs.size>1 else 0.0; b=1.0
    else:
       a=float(zs[bi-1]); b=float(zs[bi+1])
    if b - a > 1e-7:
       gr=0.6180339887498949
       c1 = a + (1.0-gr)*(b-a)
       c2 = a + gr*(b-a)
       f1=coord_eval(c1,...); f2=coord_eval(c2,...)
       # golden assumes unimodal; if not, may converge weird. 35 iterations.
       for _ in range(35):
          if f1 < f2:
             b = c2; c2 = c1; f2=f1; c1 = a + (1.0-gr)*(b-a); f1=coord_eval(c1,...)
          else:
             a = c1; c1 = c2; f1=f2; c2 = a + gr*(b-a); f2=coord_eval(c2,...)
       zm=0.5*(a+b); fm=coord_eval(zm,...)
       if fm < best_val:
          best_z=zm; best_val=fm
    # final compare endpoint? grid includes.
    return best_z
```
If `zs` size 1? grid at least >1. Good.
The golden with nonunimodal might move bracket to local; okay.
Potential expensive: `coord_eval` allocates arr. For high n and many coordinates, okay.

`coord_eval` if `mu` 0.5? The objective penalized; if `mu` small, val max + small.

Now, let's test small logic by mental:
- `coordinate_search` gradient: If `diff` is zero, `coord_eval` returns c.max(). If grid includes old, best_z maybe old. In coordinate_descent, if z==old no update. If c changes due to previous coordinate, old not necessarily optimal.

When updating c in coordinate_descent: we add diff*s, and center adjustment. But `s` includes contributions for all lags from slope at old neighbors. If z changes, c0 update correct.        val = float(vals.max())

```
def top_active_by_value(c, top_r):
    if top_r >= c.size:
       return np.arange(c.size, dtype=int)
    part = np.argpartition(-c, top_r - 1)[:top_r]
    # sort small
    order = np.argsort(-c[part])
    return part[order].astype(int)
```

### optimize_h_active code details
```
def optimize_h_active(h,n,deadline, alphas=None, top_ks=None, mus=None, maxits=None):
    h = project_h(h,n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=eval_cmax_h(h,n)
    if alphas is None:
       alphas, top_ks, mus, maxits = get_active_stages(n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    m = min(len(alphas), len(top_ks), len(mus), len(maxits))
    for st in range(m):
       rem=deadline-time.time()
       if rem < 20: break
       alpha=alphas[st]; top_r=top_ks[st]; mu=mus[st]
       it = maxits[st]
       # scale it by remaining to avoid overrun
       if n <= 800:
           it = min(it, max(30, int(rem*20)))
       else:
           it = min(it, max(15, int(rem*4)))
       if it < 10: continue
       cvec,_ = compute_cvec_h(best_h,n)
       active = select_active(cvec, top_r, 0.005)
       if active.size == 0: break
       try:
          res = minimize(...)
          cand=project_h(res.x,n)
          cc=eval_cmax_h(cand,n)
          if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
             best_c=cc; best_h=cand
       except Exception:
          pass
    return best_h,best_c
```
The it scaling with rem may use huge if rem large (1100*20=22000) but capped by maxit. For n<=800 maxit <=150, fine. For n>800 maxit<=80. Good.
`maxfun = it*10` maybe up to1500. Could be large; set `maxfun=min(it*8, max(100, int(rem*40)))`? But time. Keep `maxfun=it*8`, `maxls=15`, `maxcor=20`. For n=2048 it=80 ->640 fun. okay.
`ftol=1e-18, gtol=1e-7`.

### coordinate descent
```
def coordinate_descent_h(h,n,deadline,mu=0.001,max_passes=20,grid_points=21,rng=None):
   h=project_h(h,n); best_h=h.copy(); best_c=eval_cmax_h(h,n)
   if n==0: return...
   c,cmax=compute_cvec_h(h,n)
   sumh=float(h.sum()); target=0.5*n; center=n-1; dx=2.0/n
   grid=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,grid_points)
   top_r = min(64, max(16, n//30))
   passes=0
   last_best=best_c
   while time.time() < deadline-3 and passes < max_passes:
       if rng is not None: order=rng.permutation(n)
       else:
          order=np.arange(n)
          if passes %2==1: order=order[::-1]
       for i in order:
          rem=deadline-time.time()
          if rem < 1.0: break
          old_global=float(c.max())
          old=float(h[i])
          s=lag_slope_vec(i,h,n)
          if n <= 1000:
             z=coordinate_search(c,s,old,center,dx,sumh-old,target,mu,grid,True,None)
             zfull=True
          else:
             active=top_active_by_value(c, top_r)
             z=coordinate_search(c,s,old,center,dx,sumh-old,target,mu,grid,False,active)
             zfull=False
          if abs(z-old) > 1e-12:
             diff=z-old; center_old=float(c[center])
             c += diff*s
             c[center] = center_old + dx*(z*(1-z)-old*(1-old))
             h[i]=z; sumh += diff
             new_global=float(c.max())
             tol = 1e-10 + 1e-8*max(1.0,abs(old_global))
             if new_global > old_global + tol:
                # if was full and still worsened, revert; if active, fallback full
                # Tentatively revert
                c -= diff*s
                c[center] = center_old
                h[i]=old; sumh -= diff
                if not zfull:
                   z=coordinate_search(... full=True ...)
                   if abs(z-old)>1e-12:
                       diff2=z-old; center_old2=float(c[center])
                       c += diff2*s
                       c[center] = center_old2 + dx*(z*(1-z)-old*(1-old))
                       h[i]=z; sumh += diff2
                       new_global2=float(c.max())
                       if new_global2 <= old_global + tol:
                          pass accept
                       else:
                          c -= diff2*s; c[center]=center_old2; h[i]=old; sumh -= diff2
                        else:
                          pass accept
       passes +=1
       h_proj=project_h(h,n)
       c,cmax=compute_cvec_h(h_proj,n)
       h=h_proj; sumh=float(h.sum())
       exact=eval_cmax_h(h,n)
       if exact < best_c - 1e-15:
           best_c=exact; best_h=h.copy()
       if exact > best_c + 1e-7 and passes > 1:
           h=best_h.copy()
           c,cmax=compute_cvec_h(h,n)
           sumh=float(h.sum())
       # Stop if best_c below? no target.
       # If no exact improve for a few passes and time? continue
   return best_h,best_c
```
Need syntax for nested if. Be careful.

If active search worsens, fallback full might be expensive but only for that coordinate if worsen. Could be frequent if active set poor. But top_r large. If many, time blow. Add a counter: fallback full at most e.g. 50 per pass. If exceeded, skip active updates that worsen? Or just revert. We'll add `fallback_limit = 20` per pass; if exceeded, no full fallback, just revert active if worsened.
```
fallbacks=0
...
if not zfull and fallbacks < 20: ... fallbacks +=1 else revert
```
Good.

For n<=1000 full, if new_global worsens, revert. This may prevent improvements that require temporary increase? Full one-coordinate exact should not require. Good.

`coordinate_search` golden assumes function unimodal; for full with center concave maybe not. But if it finds z with `val` lower local; then coordinate_descent may reject if global worsens. It might miss global decreases. But grid includes points; golden around best grid. Maybe okay.
Can increase grid points for full small n to 33, gold 40. For high active, function over ~64 affine lines + center, still piecewise linear; grid enough. Use grid_points parameter.

`coord_eval_fast` if active not None: `vals = c_a + diff*s_a`; if c_a length 0 (should not), val = center_new. active top at least 1.

`make_smooth_random_h(n, rng, m)`:
```
def make_smooth_random_h(n,rng,m):
   x=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,m)
   ctrl=1.5*np.tanh(rng.randn(m))
   # Add a global offset random to allow different mean before projection
   ctrl += rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)
   xs=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
   q=np.interp(xs,x,ctrl)
   q -= q.mean()
   # scale to avoid all saturation? optional
   amax=np.max(np.abs(q))
   if amax>1: q/=amax
   h=0.5*(1+q)
   return project_h(h,n)
```
`make_periodic_h`:
```
def make_periodic_h(n,rng):
   T=float(rng.choice([2.0,2.5,3.0,3.5,4.0,5.0,6.0,8.0]))
   w=2*np.pi/T
   xs=...
   q = rng.uniform(0.5,1.0)*np.cos(w*xs+rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   q += rng.uniform(0,0.8)*np.sin(2*w*xs+rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   q += rng.uniform(0,0.5)*np.cos(3*w*xs+rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   q -= q.mean()
   amax=max(1.0,float(np.max(np.abs(q))))
   q/=amax
   h=0.5*(1+q)
   return project_h(h,n)
```
`make_random_binary_h` as above.

Detailed coordinate code:
```
def coordinate_descent_h(h,n,deadline,mu=0.001,max_passes=20,grid_points=21,rng=None):
    h = project_h(h,n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = eval_cmax_h(h,n)
    c, _ = compute_cvec_h(h,n)
    target = 0.5 * n
    center = n - 1
    dx = 2.0 / n
    sumh = float(h.sum())
    grid = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, grid_points)
    top_r = min(64, max(16, n // 30))
    passes = 0
    while time.time() < deadline - 5 and passes < max_passes:
        if rng is not None:
            order = rng.permutation(n)
        else:
            order = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
            if passes % 2 == 1:
                order = order[::-1]
        fallbacks = 0
        for ii in range(n):
            if time.time() > deadline - 2:
                break
            i = int(order[ii])
            old_global = float(c.max())
            old = float(h[i])
            s = lag_slope_vec(i,h,n)
            if n <= 1000:
                z = coordinate_search(c,s,old,center,dx,sumh-old,target,mu,grid,True,None)
                full_attempt = True
            else:
                active = top_active_by_value(c, top_r)
                z = coordinate_search(...,False,active)
                full_attempt = False
            if abs(z-old) > 1e-12:
                diff = z - old
                center_old = float(c[center])
                # update
                c += diff * s
                c[center] = center_old + dx * (z*(1.0-z) - old*(1.0-old))
                h[i] = z
                sumh += diff
                new_global = float(c.max())
                tol = 1e-10 + 1e-8 * max(1.0, abs(old_global))
                if new_global > old_global + tol:
                    # revert current update
                    c -= diff * s
                    c[center] = center_old
                    h[i] = old
                    sumh -= diff
                    if not full_attempt and fallbacks < 20:
                        fallbacks += 1
                        z2 = coordinate_search(...,True,None)
                        if abs(z2-old) > 1e-12:
                            diff2 = z2 - old
                            center_old2 = float(c[center])
                            c += diff2 * s
                            c[center] = center_old2 + dx * (z2*(1.0-z2) - old*(1.0-old))
                            h[i] = z2
                            sumh += diff2
                            new_global2 = float(c.max())
                            if new_global2 > old_global + tol:
                                c -= diff2 * s
                                c[center] = center_old2
                                h[i] = old
                                sumh -= diff2
        passes += 1
        h = project_h(h,n)
        c, _ = compute_cvec_h(h,n)
        sumh = float(h.sum())
        exact = eval_cmax_h(h,n)
        if exact < best_c - 1e-15:
            best_c = exact
            best_h = h.copy()
        elif exact > best_c + 1e-7 and passes > 1:
            h = best_h.copy()
            c, _ = compute_cvec_h(h,n)
            sumh = float(h.sum())
    return best_h, best_c
```
If exact equal best, continue. If best_h reset, the pass loop continues from best. Good.

Potential performance: `old_global = float(c.max())` and `new_global = float(c.max())` each coordinate O(n). For n=2048 and 2 passes, 8M; okay. For n=4096 and 3 passes, 12M? Actually 4096*2*3*4096=100M, okay.
`lag_slope_vec` alloc arrays and s length 2n per coordinate. For n=4096, 8191*4096*3=100M zeros, okay maybe 1-2s. Good.

`coordinate_search` active not full uses `c_a = c[active]`, `s_a=s[active]`. If active length top_r=64, small. For full n<=1000 length ~2000; grid+gold 50 eval =>100k ops per coordinate, 1000 coords=100M per pass, maybe okay for n<=1000 few passes.
For high n active length ~64, coordinate_search fast; c update dominates.

Now, `coord_eval_fast` when full creates new array `arr = c + diff * s` length up to 2000 for full; okay. When active, creates small array `vals = c_a + diff*s_a`. It doesn't update center if center included? It maxes with center_new. Good.
But if active is not full and doesn't include many max lines, golden may find z that lowers active but raises global; fallback handles. Good.

```
def smooth_max_values(vals, alpha):
    vals = np.asarray(vals, dtype=float)
    if vals.size == 0: return 0.0, np.empty(0)
    mx = float(vals.max())
    sh = alpha * (vals - mx)
    smx = float(sh.max())
    if smx > 400:
       e = np.exp(sh - smx)
       val = mx + (smx + float(np.log(e.sum()))) / alpha
    else:
       e = np.exp(sh)
       val = mx + float(np.log(e.sum())) / alpha
    w = e / e.sum()
    return float(val), w
```

```python
import time
import math
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_LAG_CACHE = {}


def get_lag_arrays(n):
    arr = _LAG_CACHE.get(int(n))
    if arr is None:
        center = int(n) - 1
        idx = np.arange(2 * int(n) - 1, dtype=int) - center
        d = np.abs(idx)
        N = (n - d).astype(float)
        pos = idx > 0
        neg = idx < 0
        arr = (d, N, pos, neg, center)
        _LAG_CACHE[int(n)] = arr
    return arr


def project_box_mean(v, low, high, target):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float).copy()
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v

    low = float(low)
    high = float(high)
    target = float(target)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.5 * (low + high), posinf=high, neginf=low)

    minsum = n * low
    maxsum = n * high
    if target <= minsum + 1e-15:
        return np.full(n, low, dtype=float)
    if target >= maxsum - 1e-15:
        return np.full(n, high, dtype=float)

    lo = float(v.min()) - high - 1.0
    hi = float(v.max()) - low + 1.0

    step = high - low + 1.0
    for _ in range(8):
        sl = np.clip(v - lo, low, high).sum()
        if sl >= target:
            break
        lo -= step
    for _ in range(8):
        sh = np.clip(v - hi, low, high).sum()
        if sh <= target:
            break
        hi += step

    for _ in range(70):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = np.clip(v - mid, low, high).sum()
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    q = np.clip(v - 0.5 * (lo + hi), low, high)

    for _ in range(8):
        res = target - float(q.sum())
        if abs(res) <= 1e-14:
            break
        if res > 0:
            cand = np.where(q < high - 1e-14)[0]
            if cand.size == 0:
                break
            add = res / float(cand.size)
            max_add = float(np.min(high - q[cand]))
            if add > max_add:
                add = 0.999 * max_add
            if add <= 1e-16:
                break
            q[cand] += add
        else:
            cand = np.where(q > low + 1e-14)[0]
            if cand.size == 0:
                break
            add = res / float(cand.size)
            max_add = float(np.min(q[cand] - low))
            if add > max_add:
                add = 0.999 * max_add
            if add <= 1e-16:
                break
            q[cand] += add

    return np.clip(q, low, high)


def project_h(h, n):
    return project_box_mean(h, 0.0, 1.0, 0.5 * n)


def eval_cmax_h(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = int(len(h))
    if n <= 0:
        return 0.0
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    if n == 1:
        return float(max(0.0, h[0] * (1.0 - h[0])) * dx)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full") * dx
    return float(np.max(c))


def compute_cvec_h(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = int(len(h))
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=float), 0.0

    d, N, pos, neg, center = get_lag_arrays(n)

    q = 2.0 * h - 1.0
    S = np.correlate(q, q, mode="full")

    P = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=float)
    P[0] = 0.0
    if n > 0:
        P[1:] = np.cumsum(q)
    Q = float(P[n])

    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    c[center] = (float(n) - S[center]) / (2.0 * float(n))

    if pos.any():
        dp = d[pos]
        c[pos] = (N[pos] + P[n - dp] + P[dp] - Q - S[pos]) / (2.0 * float(n))
    if neg.any():
        dn = d[neg]
        c[neg] = (N[neg] - P[n - dn] - P[dn] + Q - S[neg]) / (2.0 * float(n))

    cmax = float(c.max())
    return c, cmax


def smooth_max_values(vals, alpha):
    vals = np.asarray(vals, dtype=float)
    if vals.size == 0:
        return 0.0, np.empty(0, dtype=float)
    mx = float(vals.max())
    sh = float(alpha) * (vals - mx)
    smx = float(sh.max())
    if smx > 400.0:
        e = np.exp(sh - smx)
        val = mx + (smx + float(np.log(e.sum()))) / float(alpha)
    else:
        e = np.exp(sh)
        val = mx + float(np.log(e.sum())) / float(alpha)
    w = e / e.sum()
    return float(val), w


def select_active(c, top_r, tol_frac=0.004):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    threshold = cmax - max(1e-10, float(tol_frac) * max(1.0, abs(cmax)))
    active = np.flatnonzero(c >= threshold)
    if active.size > int(top_r):
        order = np.argsort(-c[active])
        active = active[order[:int(top_r)]]
    if active.size == 0:
        active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
    return active.astype(int)


def top_active_by_value(c, top_r):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float)
    if c.size <= int(top_r):
        return np.arange(c.size, dtype=int)
    part = np.argpartition(-c, int(top_r) - 1)[:int(top_r)]
    order = np.argsort(-c[part])
    return part[order].astype(int)


def active_h_obj(x, n, active, alpha, mu):
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(x, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    c, _ = compute_cvec_h(x, n)
    val, _ = smooth_max_values(c[active], alpha)
    if mu != 0.0:
        s = float(x.sum()) - 0.5 * float(n)
        val += 0.5 * float(mu) * s * s
    return float(val)


def active_h_grad(x, n, active, alpha, mu):
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(x, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    c, _ = compute_cvec_h(x, n)
    _, w = smooth_max_values(c[active], alpha)
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if w is None or w.size == 0:
        return g

    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    center = int(n) - 1

    for t in range(int(active.size)):
        wt = float(w[t])
        if wt <= 1e-15:
            continue
        k = int(active[t]) - center
        if k == 0:
            g += wt * (1.0 - 2.0 * x) * dx
        elif k > 0:
            nv = n - k
            g[:nv] += wt * (1.0 - x[k:]) * dx
            g[k:] += -wt * x[:nv] * dx
        else:
            dd = -k
            nv = n - dd
            g[:nv] += -wt * x[dd:] * dx
            g[dd:] += wt * (1.0 - x[:nv]) * dx

    if mu != 0.0:
        g += float(mu) * (float(x.sum()) - 0.5 * float(n))

    return g


def get_active_stages(n):
    if int(n) <= 800:
        alphas = [50.0, 100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 3000.0, 10000.0, 30000.0]
        top_ks = [8, 10, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28]
        mus = [0.5, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.01, 0.005, 0.001]
        maxits = [60, 80, 100, 120, 120, 140, 160]
    else:
        alphas = [50.0, 100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 3000.0, 10000.0, 30000.0]
        top_ks = [8, 12, 16, 20, 28, 36, 48]
        mus = [0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005, 0.001]
        maxits = [25, 35, 45, 55, 60, 70, 80]
    return alphas, top_ks, mus, maxits


def optimize_h_active(h, n, deadline, alphas=None, top_ks=None, mus=None, maxits=None):
    n = int(n)
    h = project_h(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = eval_cmax_h(best_h, n)

    if n <= 1:
        return best_h, best_c

    if alphas is None:
        alphas, top_ks, mus, maxits = get_active_stages(n)

    m = min(len(alphas), len(top_ks), len(mus), len(maxits))
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n

    for st in range(m):
        rem = deadline - time.time()
        if rem < 20.0:
            break

        alpha = float(alphas[st])
        top_r = int(top_ks[st])
        mu = float(mus[st])
        it = int(maxits[st])

        if n <= 800:
            it = min(it, max(20, int(rem * 20)))
        else:
            it = min(it, max(8, int(rem * 4)))
        if it < 5:
            continue

        cvec, _ = compute_cvec_h(best_h, n)
        active = select_active(cvec, top_r, 0.005)
        if active.size == 0:
            break

        maxfun = max(50, 8 * it)
        try:
            res = minimize(
                active_h_obj,
                best_h,
                jac=active_h_grad,
                args=(n, active, alpha, mu),
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                bounds=bounds,
                options={
                    "maxiter": it,
                    "ftol": 1e-20,
                    "gtol": 1e-8,
                    "maxfun": maxfun,
                    "maxls": 20,
                    "maxcor": 25,
                },
            )
            cand = project_h(res.x, n)
            cc = eval_cmax_h(cand, n)
            if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
                best_c = cc
                best_h = cand
        except Exception:
            pass

    return best_h, best_c


def lag_slope_vec(i, h, n):
    n = int(n)
    i = int(i)
    s = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    center = n - 1
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)

    if i + 1 < n:
        dpos = np.arange(1, n - i, dtype=int)
        hpos = h[i + 1:n]
        s[center + dpos] += (1.0 - hpos) * dx
        s[center - dpos] += -hpos * dx

    if i > 0:
        dneg = np.arange(1, i + 1, dtype=int)
        hneg = h[i - 1::-1]
        s[center + dneg] += -hneg * dx
        s[center - dneg] += (1.0 - hneg) * dx

    s[center] = 0.0
    return s


def coord_eval_fast(z, c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, c_a=None, s_a=None):
    diff = float(z) - float(old)
    if c_a is None:
        arr = c + diff * s
        arr[center] = c[center] + dx * (z * (1.0 - z) - old * (1.0 - old))
        val = float(arr.max())
    else:
        vals = c_a + diff * s_a
        if vals.size == 0:
            val = float(c[center] + dx * (z * (1.0 - z) - old * (1.0 - old)))
        else:
            val = float(vals.max())
        cn = float(c[center] + dx * (z * (1.0 - z) - old * (1.0 - old)))
        if cn > val:
            val = cn

    if mu != 0.0:
        ss = float(sum_rest) + float(z) - float(target)
        val += 0.5 * float(mu) * ss * ss
    return val


def coordinate_search(c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, grid, full, active=None):
    old = float(old)
    zs = np.asarray(grid, dtype=float).copy()
    extras = [old]
    for dlt in (0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20):
        zz = old + dlt
        if 0.0 < zz < 1.0:
            extras.append(zz)
        zz = old - dlt
        if 0.0 < zz < 1.0:
            extras.append(zz)

    zs = np.unique(np.concatenate((zs, np.asarray(extras, dtype=float))))
    zs = zs[(zs >= 0.0) & (zs <= 1.0)]
    if zs.size == 0:
        return old

    if full:
        c_a = None
        s_a = None
    else:
        active = np.asarray(active, dtype=int)
        c_a = c[active]
        s_a = s[active]

    vals = np.empty(zs.size, dtype=float)
    for k in range(zs.size):
        vals[k] = coord_eval_fast(zs[k], c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, c_a, s_a)

    bi = int(np.argmin(vals))
    best_z = float(zs[bi])
    best_val = float(vals[bi])

    if bi <= 0:
        a = 0.0
        b = float(zs[1]) if zs.size > 1 else 1.0
    elif bi >= zs.size - 1:
        a = float(zs[-2]) if zs.size > 1 else 0.0
        b = 1.0
    else:
        a = float(zs[bi - 1])
        b = float(zs[bi + 1])

    if b - a > 1e-7:
        gr = 0.6180339887498949
        c1 = a + (1.0 - gr) * (b - a)
        c2 = a + gr * (b - a)
        f1 = coord_eval_fast(c1, c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, c_a, s_a)
        f2 = coord_eval_fast(c2, c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, c_a, s_a)

        for _ in range(38):
            if f1 < f2:
                b = c2
                c2 = c1
                f2 = f1
                c1 = a + (1.0 - gr) * (b - a)
                f1 = coord_eval_fast(c1, c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, c_a, s_a)
            else:
                a = c1
                c1 = c2
                f1 = f2
                c2 = a + gr * (b - a)
                f2 = coord_eval_fast(c2, c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, c_a, s_a)

        zm = 0.5 * (a + b)
        fm = coord_eval_fast(zm, c, s, old, center, dx, sum_rest, target, mu, c_a, s_a)
        if fm < best_val:
            best_z = zm
            best_val = fm

    return float(best_z)


def coordinate_descent_h(h, n, deadline, mu=0.001, max_passes=20, grid_points=21, rng=None):
    n = int(n)
    h = project_h(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = eval_cmax_h(h, n)

    c, _ = compute_cvec_h(h, n)
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    center = n - 1
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    sumh = float(h.sum())
    grid = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(grid_points))

    top_r = min(64, max(16, n // 30))
    passes = 0

    while time.time() < deadline - 5.0 and passes < int(max_passes):
        if rng is not None:
            order = rng.permutation(n)
        else:
            order = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
            if passes % 2 == 1:
                order = order[::-1]

        fallbacks = 0
        for ii in range(n):
            if time.time() > deadline - 2.0:
                break
            i = int(order[ii])

            old_global = float(c.max())
            old = float(h[i])
            s = lag_slope_vec(i, h, n)

            if n <= 1000:
                z = coordinate_search(c, s, old, center, dx, sumh - old, target, mu, grid, True, None)
                full_attempt = True
            else:
                active = top_active_by_value(c, top_r)
                z = coordinate_search(c, s, old, center, dx, sumh - old, target, mu, grid, False, active)
                full_attempt = False

            if abs(z - old) > 1e-12:
                diff = float(z) - old
                center_old = float(c[center])

                c += diff * s
                c[center] = center_old + dx * (z * (1.0 - z) - old * (1.0 - old))
                h[i] = z
                sumh += diff

                new_global = float(c.max())
                tol = 1e-10 + 1e-8 * max(1.0, abs(old_global))

                if new_global > old_global + tol:
                    c -= diff * s
                    c[center] = center_old
                    h[i] = old
                    sumh -= diff

                    if not full_attempt and fallbacks < 20:
                        fallbacks += 1
                        z2 = coordinate_search(c, s, old, center, dx, sumh - old, target, mu, grid, True, None)
                        if abs(z2 - old) > 1e-12:
                            diff2 = float(z2) - old
                            center_old2 = float(c[center])

                            c += diff2 * s
                            c[center] = center_old2 + dx * (z2 * (1.0 - z2) - old * (1.0 - old))
                            h[i] = z2
                            sumh += diff2

                            new_global2 = float(c.max())
                            if new_global2 > old_global + tol:
                                c -= diff2 * s
                                c[center] = center_old2
                                h[i] = old
                                sumh -= diff2

        passes += 1

        h = project_h(h, n)
        c, _ = compute_cvec_h(h, n)
        sumh = float(h.sum())
        exact = eval_cmax_h(h, n)

        if exact < best_c - 1e-15:
            best_c = exact
            best_h = h.copy()
        elif exact > best_c + 1e-7 and passes > 1:
            h = best_h.copy()
            c, _ = compute_cvec_h(h, n)
            sumh = float(h.sum())

    return best_h, best_c


def make_random_binary_h(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=float)
    if n % 2 == 1:
        n += 1
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if n > 1:
        idx = rng.choice(n, size=n // 2, replace=False)
        h[idx] = 1.0
    else:
        h[0] = 0.5
    return project_h(h, n)


def make_smooth_random_h(n, rng, m):
    n = int(n)
    m = max(4, int(m))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, m)
    ctrl = 1.5 * np.tanh(rng.randn(m))
    ctrl += rng.uniform(-0.5, 0.5)

    xs = (np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n))
    q = np.interp(xs, x, ctrl)
    q -= q.mean()
    amax = float(np.max(np.abs(q)))
    if amax > 1.0:
        q /= amax
    h = 0.5 * (1.0 + q)
    return project_h(h, n)


def make_periodic_h(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    T = float(rng.choice([2.0, 2.5, 3.0, 3.5, 4.0, 5.0, 6.0, 8.0]))
    w = 2.0 * np.pi / T
    xs = (np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n))

    q = rng.uniform(0.5, 1.0) * np.cos(w * xs + rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
    q += rng.uniform(0.0, 0.8) * np.sin(2.0 * w * xs + rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
    q += rng.uniform(0.0, 0.5) * np.cos(3.0 * w * xs + rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))

    q -= q.mean()
    amax = float(np.max(np.abs(q)))
    if amax <= 1e-12:
        q = rng.randn(n)
        amax = max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(q))))
    q /= max(1.0, amax)
    h = 0.5 * (1.0 + q)
    return project_h(h, n)


def upsample_h(h0, n):
    h0 = np.asarray(h0, dtype=float)
    n0 = int(h0.size)
    n = int(n)
    if n0 <= 0 or n <= 0:
        return np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    x0 = (np.arange(n0, dtype=float) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n0))
    x1 = (np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n))
    h = np.interp(x1, x0, h0, left=float(h0[0]), right=float(h0[-1]))
    return project_h(h, n)


def add_top_candidate(top, c, h, max_keep=3):
    if len(top) < int(max_keep):
        top.append((float(c), h))
        return
    max_idx = 0
    max_val = float(top[0][0])
    for j in range(1, int(max_keep)):
        v = float(top[j][0])
        if v > max_val:
            max_idx = j
            max_val = v
    if float(c) < max_val:
        top[max_idx] = (float(c), h)


def optimize_candidate(h, n, active_end, coord_end, rng):
    n = int(n)
    h = project_h(h, n)
    c = eval_cmax_h(h, n)

    if time.time() < active_end:
        alphas, top_ks, mus, maxits = get_active_stages(n)
        h2, c2 = optimize_h_active(h, n, active_end, alphas, top_ks, mus, maxits)
        if c2 < c:
            h, c = h2, c2

    if time.time() < coord_end:
        if n <= 700:
            passes = 16
            grid_pts = 25
            mu = 0.004
        elif n <= 1800:
            passes = 8
            grid_pts = 17
            mu = 0.002
        elif n <= 3500:
            passes = 5
            grid_pts = 13
            mu = 0.0012
        else:
            passes = 3
            grid_pts = 11
            mu = 0.0008
        h2, c2 = coordinate_descent_h(h, n, coord_end, mu, passes, grid_pts, rng)
        if c2 < c:
            h, c = h2, c2

    return h, c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + budget_s - 10.0
    if deadline < t0 + 20.0:
        deadline = t0 + max(20.0, budget_s - 2.0)

    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed))

    try:
        h_parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
    except Exception:
        h_parent = None

    if h_parent is None or h_parent.size <= 0:
        h_parent = make_random_binary_h(512, rng)

    h_parent = project_h(h_parent, len(h_parent))
    best_h = h_parent.copy()
    best_n = int(h_parent.size)
    best_c = eval_cmax_h(best_h, best_n)

    phase1_end = t0 + min(85.0, 0.10 * budget_s)
    phase2_end = t0 + min(610.0, 0.58 * budget_s)
    phase3_end = t0 + min(890.0, 0.82 * budget_s)
    final_end = deadline

    if budget_s > 120.0 and best_n <= 1200:
        h1, c1 = optimize_h_active(best_h, best_n, phase1_end)
        if c1 < best_c:
            best_c = c1
            best_h = h1
        if time.time() < phase1_end - 8.0:
            h2, c2 = coordinate_descent_h(
                h1,
                best_n,
                phase1_end,
                mu=0.004,
                max_passes=8,
                grid_points=25,
                rng=rng,
            )
            if c2 < best_c:
                best_c = c2
                best_h = h2

    if budget_s >= 800.0:
        main_n = 2048
    elif budget_s >= 400.0:
        main_n = 1536
    else:
        main_n = max(768, min(1024, int(best_n) * 2 if int(best_n) > 500 else 1024))
    if main_n % 2 == 1:
        main_n += 1

    if time.time() < phase2_end - 80.0:
        starts = []
        starts.append(upsample_h(best_h, main_n))
        for _ in range(3):
            starts.append(make_random_binary_h(main_n, rng))
        for m in (8, 16, 32):
            starts.append(make_smooth_random_h(main_n, rng, m))
        starts.append(make_periodic_h(main_n, rng))

        top = []
        for hh in starts:
            if time.time() > phase2_end - 70.0:
                break
            hh = project_h(hh, main_n)
            cc = eval_cmax_h(hh, main_n)
            if cc < best_c:
                best_c = cc
                best_h = hh.copy()
                best_n = main_n
            add_top_candidate(top, cc, hh, 3)

        if len(top) > 0:
            cand_start = time.time()
            cand_end = min(phase2_end - 25.0, deadline - 50.0)
            if cand_end > cand_start + 40.0:
                cnt = len(top)
                for s in range(cnt):
                    cend = cand_start + float(s + 1) * (cand_end - cand_start) / float(cnt)
                    if cend > deadline - 50.0:
                        cend = deadline - 50.0
                    if time.time() >= cend - 10.0:
                        break

                    h0 = project_h(top[s][1], main_n)
                    c0 = float(top[s][0])

                    rem = max(20.0, cend - time.time())
                    act_end = time.time() + 0.58 * rem
                    coord_end = max(act_end, cend - 5.0)

                    h2, c2 = optimize_candidate(h0, main_n, act_end, coord_end, rng)
                    if c2 < best_c:
                        best_c = c2
                        best_h = h2.copy()
                        best_n = main_n

    if budget_s > 800.0 and best_n < 4096 and time.time() < phase3_end - 130.0:
        ultra_n = 4096
        h_up = upsample_h(best_h, ultra_n)
        cu = eval_cmax_h(h_up, ultra_n)
        if cu < best_c:
            best_c = cu
            best_h = h_up.copy()
            best_n = ultra_n

        rem = max(0.0, phase3_end - time.time())
        if rem > 120.0:
            act_end = time.time() + 0.45 * rem
            coord_end = max(act_end, phase3_end - 8.0)
            h2, c2 = optimize_candidate(h_up, ultra_n, act_end, coord_end, rng)
            if c2 < best_c:
                best_c = c2
                best_h = h2.copy()
                best_n = ultra_n

    if time.time() < final_end - 30.0:
        rem = max(0.0, final_end - time.time())
        if rem > 30.0:
            act_end = time.time() + min(55.0, 0.35 * rem)
            h2, c2 = optimize_h_active(best_h, best_n, act_end)
            if c2 < best_c:
                best_c = c2
                best_h = h2
                best_n = int(len(h2))

        if time.time() < final_end - 12.0:
            if best_n <= 1000:
                h3, c3 = coordinate_descent_h(
                    best_h,
                    best_n,
                    final_end - 2.0,
                    mu=0.001,
                    max_passes=14,
                    grid_points=25,
                    rng=rng,
                )
            else:
                h3, c3 = coordinate_descent_h(
                    best_h,
                    best_n,
                    final_end - 2.0,
                    mu=0.0005,
                    max_passes=4,
                    grid_points=13,
                    rng=rng,
                )
            if c3 < best_c:
                best_c = c3
                best_h = h3
                best_n = int(len(h3))

    best_h = project_h(best_h, best_n)
    best_c = eval_cmax_h(best_h, best_n)

    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```
